## Step 2 — 2026-09-10T11:23:29.721670+00:00

Step 2 built precise mathematical objects from the Step 1 state, the Strategist's sign-blind insight, and S0910A01's quantitative analysis. The main construction is the complete per-sign Einstein census for dimension 4, formalized as Lemma 1 (sign-blind CGB: δ_σ < 1/(1+√3) forces 2χ > 3|τ|, sign-blind) and Lemma 2 (LeBrun negative Einstein exclusion via CGB + SW: for M = X#k\overline{CP²} with 0 < k < c₁²(X), the combination 4∫|W⁺|² = 8π²(A-k) - 16λ²Vol/3 and 16λ²Vol ≥ 32π²(A-k) forces ∫|W⁺|² < 0). I showed the three Einstein signs are excluded by fundamentally different mechanisms: λ>0 by gauge-theoretic SW (incompatible with positive Ricci), λ=0 by topology (K3/T⁴ holonomy constraints), λ<0 by integral curvature (CGB+SW). Crucially, the λ<0 mechanism's integral nature means it only weakly constrains δ₋ on non-Einstein metrics: the |E|² term absorbs the negative quantity that creates the Einstein contradiction.

I verified S0910A01's δ₋ lower bound numerically: for varying LeBrun manifolds with A→∞ and k≈2A/3, δ_lb ∼ 1/√A → 0 (script lebrun_delta_bound.py). Raised a basic-class-square discrepancy with S0910A01 in message #204: all SW basic classes of M have κ² = A-k (not A), so the Einstein exclusion holds for all k < A, and the δ bound derivation needs clarification.

Built the dimension ≥ 5 assessment: no closed manifold of dim ≥ 5 is definitively known to admit no Einstein metric. This makes high-dimensional refutation of Q_n depend on solving a strictly harder open problem. Combined with the positive-branch protection (all known no-Einstein 4-manifolds have δ₊ ≥ 1) and the negative branch dichotomy (noncollapsed → ALE bubbles with order-1 defect, or collapse), the evidence tilts toward Q_n true.

The new Berger sphere computation (berger_collapse_ricci.py) shows that Hopf-fiber collapse creates order-1 Ricci anisotropy in all dimensions: Ric_vertical → 0 while Ric_horizontal → 2(n+1) as t → 0, giving δ₊ → 1. This directly supports the 'no bad collapse' argument for P_n⁺.

Saved the full construction note in workspace/notes/step2_construction_sign_census_dichotomy.md. Sent findings to peer group as messages #202 and #204.

### Continuation

(1) COLLAPSE ANISOTROPY GENERALIZATION: Extend the Berger sphere result to more general collapsing models. Compute Ricci eigenvalues for: (a) circle bundles over surfaces with varying fiber size, (b) torus bundles (T² fiber) with anisotropic collapse, (c) infranil fibers, (d) Cheeger deformations. The goal is to determine whether ALL non-quotient collapse under bounded Ricci creates order-1 Ricci anisotropy, or find a counterexample (a collapsing model with δ₊ → 0). Use qiushi-yau26 tools and SageMath for curvature computations. This directly tests the 'no bad collapse' conjecture in workspace/notes/step2_construction_sign_census_dichotomy.md Section VIII.

(2) BASIC CLASS SQUARE VERIFICATION: Resolve the discrepancy with S0910A01 about κ² = A-k vs A. Compute the SW basic classes of M = X#k\overline{CP²} explicitly for small examples and verify the self-intersection numbers. This determines whether the δ₋ lower bound formula needs correction.

(3) O'NEILL RICCI SPLITTING BOUND: Use the O'Neill formulas for Riemannian submersions to derive an analytic lower bound on Ricci anisotropy for non-flat fiber collapse. If the fiber curvature scales like 1/t², what is the resulting Ricci eigenvalue splitting? Can this be made into a rigorous lower bound on δ₊ for non-quotient collapse?

(4) NEGATIVE BRANCH PROBE: Attempt to construct or rule out tight negative Ricci pinching on a concrete LeBrun manifold (small A, k). Use prescribed curvature techniques or explicit metric construction (e.g., warped products, doubly-warped products) to estimate how small δ₋ can be made.

Read the full construction note workspace/notes/step2_construction_sign_census_dichotomy.md and the Berger script workspace/scripts/berger_collapse_ricci.py. Check peer messages for S0910A01/A03 responses. The research state and active files are in workspace/notes/ and workspace/scripts/.

## Step 57 — 2026-09-11T19:34:14.031216+00:00

Step 57 completed the four-dimensional noncollapsed wall into a clean unified dichotomy. Starting from the Step-56 audit and the Strategist's direction, I built three interlocking research objects.

(1) Kähler exclusion theorem. For 1≤r≤35 in the negative (Σ₂×Σ₂)/⟨ι×ι⟩ family: each M_r contains r non-torsion +2-spheres with b₊=9+r>1. By Baraglia/Kronheimer adjunction (add handle to sphere → genus 1, then |⟨c(s),[S]⟩|+[S]²≤0 contradicts [S]²=2) any Kähler/symplectic structure gives nonzero SW by Taubes, which is impossible. In reverse orientation, 36-r≥1 +2-spheres with b₊=45-r>1. So no mixed row is Kähler/symplectic in either orientation. The same argument works verbatim for the positive (S²×S²)/Z₂ mirror: r=1,2,3 have b₊=2,3,4>1 in original and b₊=4,3,2>1 in reverse. This strictly strengthens Noether parity (which excluded only odd rows from having complex structures) and completely removes the even-row question: no mixed row can be settled by Aubin–Yau or any known Kähler existence theorem.

(2) Van Kampen proof for π₁=1. M₀ is simply connected (smooth double cover of P¹×P¹ branched over smooth connected (6,6) curve, by Catanese cyclic-cover proposition). Both ±2 disk bundles over S² are simply connected with boundary RP³. The inclusion RP³→D± induces the zero map on π₁. Van Kampen gives π₁(M_r) = π₁(W)/{same boundary images} = π₁(M₀) = 1 for all r. Consequence: all mixed rows have trivial π₁, zero simplicial volume, and all Gromov-norm/Sambusetti/BCG obstructions are vacuous.

(3) Bauer–Furuta scope check. Inverted the question: does any known mechanism produce a nonzero monopole/BF class on a mixed row? All known inputs (Kotschick Theorem 10, Ishida–LeBrun Cor 13, Bauer connected-sum nonvanishing) require Kähler/symplectic summands. Mixed rows are not Kähler and do not split along +2-spheres (boundary RP³, not S³). Result: no known mechanism reaches mixed rows. This is a status statement, not a vanishing theorem.

The multi-bubble estimate source gap was precisely identified. Ozuch cites [Biq13] O. Biquard, Désingularisation de métriques d'Einstein I, Invent. Math. 192 (2013), 197–252, Section 14 for the EH-region estimate. Two retrieval attempts failed to find the original paper. The reduction theorem should be stated as conditional on this estimate.

The higher-genus family (Σ_h×Σ_h)/⟨ι×ι⟩ was checked for h=2,...,5 and the general formula gives strict HT margin 4(h-1)²+6min(r,N-r) for all h≥2, all r. No enlargement produces a topological contradiction.

where H⁴(ζ⁺) = −(ρ(ζ)⁻∘ζ⁺)/(2r⁴) for compatible and H⁴(ζ⁻) = −(ζ⁻∘ρ(ζ)⁺)/(2r⁴) for reversed. The chirality of H⁴ is the load-bearing distinction.

## Step 100 — 2026-09-13T07:04:21.910889+00:00

Step 100 proved two results that together close the positive mirror at leading inter-node order. Starting from the Step 99 handoff which identified the adjacent-balanced scale-ratio window as a possible existence window and the uncomputed diagonal tensor coefficient q_diag as the single decisive number, I pursued both questions simultaneously.

First, the four-node exchange-closure theorem (pure algebra). In the adjacent-balanced pattern ε=(+,+,-,-), the product orbifold's adjacency structure forces nodes within each chirality class to have swapped adjacent/diagonal roles: node 1 pairs adjacently with node 3 and diagonally with node 4, while node 2 does the reverse. The single-node feasibility condition |P_j| ≤ |Q_j| at scale ratio r=u₄/u₃ for node 1 becomes the same condition at 1/r for node 2. The intersection of these reciprocal windows is nonempty iff r=1 is feasible: |c_adj+c_diag| ≤ q_adj+q_diag, numerically 0.02743 ≤ 0.00800+q_diag, requiring q_diag ≥ 0.01943. For r=1 and r=3 patterns, at least one node has only adjacent opposite sources, giving immediate obstruction since c_adj > q_adj. For diagonal balanced, all opposite pairs are adjacent—same argument. The entire positive mirror thus reduces to a single threshold on q_diag.

Second, the structural diagonal tensor vanishing. I loaded A01's step98/step96 spectral framework via importlib (the framework's compute_full_chirality already accepts 'SS' as a valid source pair; A01 simply never called it). Initial validation confirmed NS gives correct values (AA compact[0,0] = 0.008005, matching A01's stored data). The SS computation at all four convergence-ladder rungs (τ,L_max)=(0.08,80),(0.04,120),(0.02,180),(0.01,260) gave identically zero in both AA and BB sectors, all chirality blocks. Debugging confirmed this is not a loading issue: the NS computation works correctly, and the framework gives nonzero SS results at very small Lmax only if the sign structure allows it (which it does not).

The mechanism is a structural identity. In tensor_value_modes, the c2 seed at node 'S' has sign (-1)^ℓ relative to node 'N', while the s2 seed has sign -(-1)^ℓ. These are always opposite. The SO(2) rotational symmetry of the kernel plane K=ω⊥ ensures equal contribution magnitudes for c2 and s2 at each spectral level. Hence every (ℓ_t, ℓ_s) contribution cancels exactly in pairwise fashion. I verified the sign ratios explicitly at ℓ_t=2,3,4,5 for all four mode families (electric c2/s2, magnetic c2/s2): at S node, c2 and s2 always have opposite signs. Adjacent pairs survive because one factor's tensor poles match (both ratio +1), so c2+s2 reinforces.

Combined result: q_diag=0 < threshold 0.01943, so the adjacent-balanced row is obstructed. ALL five mixed patterns are obstructed at leading order. The compatible pattern has f₄≡0 by same-chirality vanishing, consistent with KE existence. The coefficient table explains the known dichotomy between KE endpoints and non-Kähler mixed rows.
